On August 13, 2025, Virginia Electric and Power Company (the “Company”) filed its 
Rebuttal Testimony in Case No. PUR-2025-00058. After filing this Rebuttal Testimony, the 
Company determined that certain non-substantive corrections needed to be made. Please find 
enclosed for electronic filing in the above-referenced matter a corrected version of the following, 
with versions in clean, and in redline for testimony pages, each which should entirely replace the 
previously-filed versions.

Weighted Average Demand F (Ln 4 * Ln 7)
As average demand, or energy, is already being considered as part of the NCPD factor, I3
do not believe that Staff Witness Watkins’ calculation regarding the A&E method shows4
what percentage of the allocation factor is governed by demand, and separately, what5
percentage is governed by energy. Instead, Staff Witness Watkins is calculating the6
percentage by which the excess demand component of the NCPD factor is reduced7
relative to the calculation of the A&E factor. My Rebuttal Schedule 10 shows proof of8
this concept for both the 2026 and 2027 forecast adjusted A&E factors. As both the A&E9
factor and the NCPD factor are calculated based on a weighted average demand factor10
and a weighted excess demand factor, and the A&E factor is more weighted to average11
demand, some portion of what is weighted to the excess demand factor in the NCPD12
factor calculation is included in the amount weighted towards average demand in the13
A&E factor calculation. As my Rebuttal Schedule 10 shows, for the 2026 factor, this14
portion is calculated as 3.9598%, the amount calculated by Staff Witness Watkins. In15
total for the 2026 factor, the A&E allocation factor is weighted 57.3980% for average16

Q. As you discussed, one concern other parties expressed with regard to the A&E4
methodology pertains to the recent and ongoing load growth in the Company’s5
service territory. Do you believe that the A&E methodology is adequately able to6
address this load growth?7
Yes. I believe that one of the advantages of the A&E methodology is that, because theA.8
weightings of average and excess are based on the system load factor, the allocation9
factor calculation will naturally adjust to reflect changes in the system composition. For10
example, in the case where there is significant growth in high load factor customers, these11
customers will create an increase in the overall system load factor, increasing the12
weighting of the average demand component of the A&E factor and decreasing the13
weighting of the excess factor. Table 6, below, shows the weighted average demand and14
weighted excess demand calculations for the A&E factor using relevant data from the15
